# ONE: build log

Moved word for word from the "Where things stand" section of `CLAUDE.md` on 2026-11-11, oldest first, so that every session stops loading it whole. New entries go at the end. The four "Decided by the owner" entries also live in "Product decisions the code assumes" of `CLAUDE.md`, which is the authoritative place for them. The last three entries are the state as it stood on 2026-11-11.

- **M0, clean and safe base: built and verified on 2026-10-08** (`npm run ci:verify` and the Playwright suite green). It is one commit on the local branch `one/m0` of this repository, whose local branches still carry the history of other products (the source of molds: `git show ab2e84d:<path>`). **ONE's own repository is `onedeveloperapp/one-platform` on GitHub** (private, the remote `origin`; created by the owner on 2026-10-21, and this machine pushes to it over SSH as the collaborator BrunoSantanaDeveloper): its history starts at one commit, `f4e0c2a`, with the tree of `1b003c3` and the deploy workflow, and none of the other products'. Its first deploy, of `f4e0c2a`, went up on 2026-10-21 with no database: the public site, served from `gru1`. Since 2026-10-26 the local branch `main` is ONE's and tracks `origin/main`, and the work is done on it: a push is `git push`, and a push to `main` deploys production (see "Platform decisions"). The old local `main` of flyee.club is `flyee-club-main` and the old chain of `one/m3` is `one/m3-history`, kept as molds: never check either out in this work tree, never commit on them, never push them (they share no history with `origin/main`).
- **M1, foundation for many firms, built on the branch `one/m1` (three sprints). Sprint S1 (database and firm):** ONE's own database baseline (`packages/db/migrations`, with a pgTAP suite in which every file has two firms); the firm, the two things a membership says (`org_role` for the firm account, `firm_function` for the work), invitations bound to a confirmed e-mail, registrations at the bar per person, and every change to a team as an audited function that takes the firm; the second factor asked by role (`second_factor_required()`, a setting) and by the functions that give or take access (`require_aal2()`); the app on top of it: sign-up that founds a firm or accepts an invitation (`/auth/complete-profile`, `/convite/[token]`), the team screen (`/settings/team`), the gate of the logged area (`lib/firm/gate.ts`), the operator console per firm (`/admin/escritorios`); `npm run check:db-refs` and the pgTAP job in CI.
- **M1, sprint S2 (vault, documents, AI) is built.** The AI layer: every run of a model is a row (`ai_runs`: the task, the prompt version, the model, the effort, a hash of the input, the output, the cost), what a task asks is an immutable published version (`prompt_versions`), and an answer waits in the firm's queue (`approvals`) until a person in a function the task names decides it with the second factor (migration `0016_ai_runs`, 52 pgTAP assertions with two firms). Only the backend starts and finishes a run; a run nobody finished is given up by a sweep (`expire_stale_ai_runs`, every 15 minutes). The executor is `runAiTask` in `packages/ai` over a store interface, with a fake provider and a fake store; the app enters through `runFirmTask` (`lib/ai/executor.ts`). The firm decides in `/aprovacoes`; the operator reads cost per firm and the measure of each task, and runs the executor check, in `/admin/ai`. The audit trail carries the kind of actor (person, support, system, ai, client), stamped by the database. One task exists, `plataforma.verificacao`, with nothing of a client in it; the consoles to publish a prompt version and to change a task's autonomy come with the first product task (M2), their functions are in the database and tested. **The vault** (`packages/connectors`, migration `0017_connections`): a firm's API tokens at its vendors are sealed in the backend with AES-256-GCM and `VAULT_KEYS`, bound to the firm and the account; no API role reads the table of sealed tokens, not even the service role, so every read goes through a function that audits it; a name that would hold a lawyer's own credential is refused in the package and in the database; the logical backup never copies it; a daily job discards what expired and reseals under a newer key. **Files and issued documents** (`packages/documents`, migration `0018_files_documents`): a firm's files live in the private bucket `firm-files`, uploaded straight from the browser after a reservation and kept only after the backend measured them (hash, size, the type their bytes say); a document ONE issues is a version with a public code, checked by anyone at `/verificar/<code>` without seeing its content. **The technical test of PDF normalization** decided for the function: photos to PDF, splitting by a size limit and compressing a scan again run with `pdf-lib` and `sharp` in seconds and about 330 MB (the numbers and what would reopen the decision are in the README of `packages/documents`). The screens that connect an account, upload a file and issue a document come with their first use (the WhatsApp connection in S3, the client's folder and the first templates in M2); the mechanisms are built and tested without them.
- **M1, sprint S3 (WhatsApp and the shell) is built.** Each firm connects its own WhatsApp number through the official Cloud API (migration `0019_whatsapp`, 55 pgTAP assertions with two firms): one webhook for every firm (`/api/webhooks/whatsapp`), each delivery authenticated by its signature over the raw body and each event routed by the number it carries to the one firm that number is active in, within the provider that signed it; everything that arrives is idempotent by the vendor's message id. What may be sent is decided in the database and nowhere else (`reserve_wa_send`: free text only inside the 24 hours after the person's last message, an approved template outside it, never a template to who opted out, nothing from a support visit), and a send is reserved, claimed and then sent, so it may fail and never goes out twice; one whose outcome nobody can tell (the vendor did not answer) is recorded as unconfirmed, never as "not sent", and is never sent again by itself. The firm's token is sealed in the vault and opened per piece of work; the media people send is brought into the firm's files; a number that also lives on the firm's phone (coexistence) has its echoes, contacts and history mirrored, and a disconnection on the phone closes it. A number outlives the connection it had (no removal of a connection takes a conversation with it), and a firm's numbers are the vendor's or the simulator's, never both. `packages/whatsapp` is pure (the webhook parser and signature, the Cloud API provider and the Embedded Signup steps, the rules of a template with the bar's advertising rules as warnings, and a simulator that stands where the vendor stands), 36 tests. The firm works in `/atendimento` (conversations, the thread, the answer the database will accept, opt-out) and connects in `/settings/connections` (the simulator, or the vendor's signup window). The shell: the menu shows each person their own items from the first paint (`lib/firm/menu.ts`, the viewer handed by the server layout), the firm's setup checklist is computed from facts (`firm_setup_facts`) and `/inicio` answers what needs somebody today (`firm_today`, migration `0020_firm_today`).
- **M2, the client and the case, in progress on the branch `one/m2`. Sprint S4 (the case and the client's folder) is built.** Reference data (migration `0021_reference`): the 27 state courts with their CNJ code, the filing system of each court for the small-claims and the civil court (seeded from a DJEN sample by `scripts/djen-systems.mjs --sql`, every row unconfirmed until checked in the court's acts), 4,008 airports (OurAirports, public domain) and the airlines that fly in Brazil. The case (migration `0022_cases`, 39 pgTAP assertions with two firms): the firm's clients (`people`, one per CPF in a firm), cases numbered per firm with their stage, who is in each (claimants, adults or minors, and the guardians of a minor), the legs of the trip in local time, the lawsuits with their forum, claimants and signing lawyers, the timeline, the documents a person of the firm accepts or refuses with a reason, and the client's link: one adult of one case, by a token `client_links` keeps only as its SHA-256 (a link sent over WhatsApp stays in that conversation, like any message), which ends by itself, when the person leaves the case or when the firm ends it; a support visit writes nothing through it; through it the client fills their data and the children's, the trip, sends files (measured by the backend, a ceiling per link) and says they finished. Every write is a function that takes the case and records an event; a support visit writes nothing. The pure rules start in `packages/rules` (the CNJ number from Resolução CNJ 65/2008, flight numbers and airports, the OAB registration, a person's age on a day). The CPF lookup has its real provider (`packages/identity`, Serpro Consulta CPF v3, which answers for a CPF with its birth date and never for a minor); the credential is the platform's (`SERPRO_*` in the server's environment), the database decides whether a lookup may happen (an adult, a daily ceiling per firm) and keeps the answer only while the data asked still holds. The app: `/casos` (the list with filters by stage, documents to check and clients who finished; opening a case, also from a conversation in `/atendimento`), `/casos/[id]` (what the case still lacks, people with the CPF lookup, the trip with the delay at arrival, documents, lawsuits with the court's known system, the summary, the client's link sent by WhatsApp inside the 24 hours, the timeline) and `/c/[token]`, the client's page with the firm's name only, no account, no referrer, no tracker, no index. `/inicio` counts the documents to check and the clients who finished.
- **M2, sprint S5, first half (signatures and acceptances) is built.** What the client signs (migration `0023_signatures`, 15 pgTAP assertions with two firms): a PDF of the case sent to adults of the case by the firm's own ZapSign account (`packages/signatures` gains its ZapSign provider; the token and a webhook secret of ONE's are sealed in the vault when a manager connects the account in `/settings/connections`; ONE points the account's webhook at `/api/webhooks/zapsign`, takes a delivery only with that secret, and reads the state again from ZapSign), by gov.br (the client downloads the PDF from their link, signs it at `assinador.iti.br` and sends it back; a person confirms) or by e-Notariado (the act's link; a person confirms). An envelope is reserved by a person and opened by the backend (`one-signatures-sweep`), never twice: one claimed and never answered is uncertain. A delivery of ZapSign is checked by the hash of the connection's secret before the vault opens; a cancel is made in ONE at once and owed to ZapSign until the backend sent it; ZapSign receives the signers' names only. The signed PDF becomes an accepted document of the case. What the client accepts: the texts for clients in force (`consent_terms` with the slugs `cliente-*`, published by the operator; none is seeded) accepted on their link, each with the text frozen and what the server saw of the request (`client_consents`). The case screen has the signatures card and the client's acceptances; the client's page has the texts to accept and the documents to sign.
- **M2, sprint S5, second half (what happens to a document) is built.** Migration `0024_document_checks` (20 pgTAP assertions with two firms): every document a client or the firm sends to a case is claimed by the backend (`one-case-documents`, a cron and the event `one/case.document.received` sent when a file joins a case; without Inngest it runs after the response; the job's steps carry document ids only, a passing failure is tried again up to the setting's attempts, and a document whose work never finishes is given up after them). A photo becomes an A4 PDF kept beside it (`normalized` purpose, `packages/documents/normalize`; also for a document a person already accepted, and never twice), and the AI checks the kinds the setting `document_checks` names while the document still waits for the firm: the first product task, `caso.documento.conferencia` (`lib/cases/documents-work.ts`; the model reads the reduced photo or the PDF and a short briefing, `lib/cases/document-check.ts`), suggests accepting or refusing it (the type it is, legible, the name on it and whether it is the person's, the reason the client would read) and reads the legs of a ticket or a boarding pass. The suggestion waits in the firm's queue for anyone who works cases; it is decided in `/aprovacoes` (a card of its own: accept with or without the legs, refuse with the reason, or turn the suggestion down) or on the case, where accepting or refusing the document with the second factor approves its check (measured as a correction when it differs) and without it decides the document and withdraws the check (nothing of the AI applied or counted). Approved, it acts in the database: the document accepted or refused, and each leg read added to the case once (a leg that does not pass the checks of a leg is left out). A document a person decided first keeps that decision and its check waits for nobody; a second check of a document never joins one that waits (it is written already withdrawn, so it never acts by itself), and deciding a document withdraws what still waits of it; approving a check whose document was decided some other way is refused; a check that did not wait (the task earned autonomy) has its answer applied as given, once (`apply_unattended_document_check`). The operator publishes the next version of a task's instructions and changes its autonomy in `/admin/ai` (the fields the code of a task reads stay required). From a conversation in `/atendimento`, a person sends a file of the firm (a PDF as a document, a photo as an image, with a caption, within the sizes the Cloud API carries, a setting) inside the 24 hours, through the same reserve, claim and send.
- **M2, sprint S6, first half (the rules of a case) is built.** The pure rules in `packages/rules`, each with its version and its cases as tables: the forum (`foro-v1`: the passenger's domicile by default, any other only in a city of an airport of the trip and with a recorded reason), the kind of proceeding (`tipo-de-processo-v1`: the adults in the small-claims court, a minor in the civil court, and the forty-minimum-wage ceiling counted per claimant), the reading of a METAR for weather signs, the Tema 1.417 triage (`tema-1417-v1`: the risk that the STF's national suspension reaches a case, from what the airline said and the weather, with its grounds named; a risk for the firm to weigh, never a decision) and the count of a lawyer's causes per state against the supplementary registration at the bar (`inscricao-suplementar-v1`). Migration `0025_case_rules` (17 pgTAP assertions with two firms) holds what they decide on: the minimum wage by the day each act took effect, what each claimant asks, what the airline told the passenger (anybody of the firm writes it) with the firm's decision, file now or hold (a partner's or a lawyer's; one left out stays as it was), the triage the rule gave (written by the backend that ran it, never by a person), the weather reports of the affected leg kept as evidence (`case_weather`, written by the backend only, each read replacing the last and recording what was asked, after a cooldown the database decides), each Brazilian airport's time offset and the municipality it sits in when it is not the city it serves, `firm_causes_by_state`, and `create_planned_proceedings` (all the planned lawsuits at once, or none). `save_proceeding` refuses, for a lawsuit still planned, a minor in the small-claims court (a claimant with a guardian and no birth date is one), a forum with no link to the flight, and a forum at the domicile in another state than its claimants'; a lawsuit the court already has is recorded as it is. "Nothing observed" is concluded only when every airport of the affected leg was read for the hours the leg has now; an airport abroad, one with no report, or a leg corrected since the read leaves the weather not assessed. A read that fails gives its wait back. `packages/flights` gains its REDEMET provider (the platform's key, `REDEMET_API_KEY`). The case screen has the card "Como este caso vai a juízo" (`casos/[id]/components/rules-card.tsx`, `lib/cases/rules.ts`, `lib/cases/rule-actions.ts`): the lawsuits the rule plans and one button to create them, what each claimant asks, and the triage with the reports read and the decision; the lawsuit dialog offers another forum only among the cities of the trip and warns when one more cause makes a lawyer's practice in a state habitual.
- **M2, sprint S6, second half (the client's account, the complaint, the certificate inventory) is built.** What the client told (migration `0026_statements`, 27 pgTAP assertions with two firms and two cases in one of them): a statement is somebody's of the case, spoken (an audio the firm got over WhatsApp, one a person of the firm uploads, or one the client sends through their link) or written. The backend transcribes the audio (task `caso.relato.transcricao`, autonomous, a Gemini model in its prompt version) and proposes the order of the facts (task `caso.relato.cronologia`, which waits in the firm's queue); a person approves it with the second factor, the client reads it on their link and confirms it or says what is wrong, and a chronology asked again reads the one before and the client's words. The worker names a run and nothing else: the database reads the run's own answer and whether it waits for a person (`record_statement_work`), each claim has a number, needed to read the statement and to say what came of it, that fences a late worker and makes each run its own; an answer enters the queue only for a statement that exists and still waits for it, and never beside another (any other is written already withdrawn), and an answer that waits is what the statement waits for, whatever its worker said. The sweep (`one-case-statements`, every 5 minutes and on `one/case.statement.received`) recomputes what is due: an audio a client's page never came back for is received or dropped, a run that waits is taken as waiting, and a passing failure waits a growing while before its next attempt. Every state has its way forward for a person: an audio that could not be transcribed is asked again or written down by hand, a chronology is asked again unless one waits in the queue or the client confirmed it, work that waits after a failure is resumed on request, and a statement the client did not confirm can be removed (what waited of it leaves the queue). A step of the jobs route now has 300 seconds, for the model that hears an audio. The transcription is a task of the AI layer, so every run is a row: the `packages/transcribe` mold of the previous product (its own table and a direct call to the model) was not recovered; what it taught is kept (a pinned model, never a `-latest` alias; a transcript may stop before the end of a long audio, so the person hears the audio beside the text and the client confirms the chronology). `packages/ai` refuses to hand an audio to a provider that would not send it to the model, and `packages/documents` tells an MP4 of sound alone from a video by its tracks (a recorder may write one under a general brand). The administrative complaint (`admin_complaints`): ONE files nothing; the firm records the channel (Anac Passageiro, consumidor.gov.br, Procon, the airline), the protocol, the dates and what came of it, and the day an answer is due is proposed from the channel's own days (a setting). The certificate inventory (`certificate_inventory`): each lawyer declares the KIND of certificate they sign with per court system, who issued it, until when, and the last day they confirmed their computer signs there; nothing of a certificate itself (a test of the catalog and one of the app hold the columns). The app: the cards "O que aconteceu" and "Reclamação antes do processo" on the case, the account and the chronology on the client's page, the account beside the proposal in `/aprovacoes`, the certificates in `/settings/team`, and two more counts in `/inicio` and `/casos` (chronologies a client contested, complaints past their day).
- **M2, sprint S7, first part (the filing package and its receipt) is built.** ONE builds the package of a lawsuit and files nothing: the lawyer files in the court's own system, with their own login. The pure rule is `@flyee/courts/filing` (`planFiling`: the petition first, then each kind of document in the order of a filing, by person; plain names with no accent or space, never repeated, within the length the setting gives; a file larger than the system's limit marked to be cut, a page that alone exceeds it named as a problem; one petition per package). Migration `0027_filing` (22 pgTAP assertions with two firms): a petition is a document of the case (kind `petition`, a PDF a person accepted); a person asks for the package of a lawsuit not filed yet that names its court and system (`request_filing_package`: the petition and the accepted documents that go, asking again replaces the one in course); the backend builds it (`one-filing-packages`, every 5 minutes and on `one/case.filing.requested`; a numbered claim, as for statements) and records its files in order (`filing_package_items`: the document's own file, or the parts cut for it under the `filing` purpose, each checked by the database: of the same firm, a PDF that opens, under the limit, each under a plain name of its own, the petition first, and every document the person chose with all its parts together and in order); a PDF that could not be opened (protected, which `packages/documents` now tells by leaving its pages uncounted, or damaged) is named as a problem and never goes as it is, a page that alone exceeds the limit is tried with its images compressed again before it is named, a passing failure waits before the next attempt, and a part cut for a package is nothing else (it is never attached to a case nor sent in a conversation, and is forgotten only when nothing names it); the limit is data (`court_systems.max_file_bytes`; Projudi of Goiás seeded with the 6 MB of the court's notice of 2020, unconfirmed, and the screen says so); a package replaced or given up forgets the parts cut for it, never the documents. A partner or a lawyer attaches the receipt the court's system gave (`attach_filing_receipt`: a file of their own that is nothing else, the case number of that court, a day that has come, for a lawsuit still planned whose court, system and documents are the ones the package was built for), which marks the lawsuit as filed through `save_proceeding` and closes the package. A lawsuit somebody records as filed by hand, or closes, and a case that ends, give up the package they had in course. The worker has its own test against the embedded database with real PDFs (`apps/web/src/lib/cases/filing.db.test.ts`, part of `npm run test:db:roundtrip`). The app: the card "Protocolo" on the case (`casos/[id]/components/filing-card.tsx`: choose the petition and the documents, the files in order with their download under the package's names, what to do in the court's system, the receipt), the firm's filing queue at `/protocolo` (by court and system) and the count of packages ready in `/inicio`.
- **M2, sprint S7, second part (the DJEN by OAB and the triage in shadow mode) is built.** `packages/courts` has the real client of the DJEN's public API (`@flyee/courts/providers/djen`, no key), written from its OpenAPI reference (read on 2026-10-08) and then checked against the API itself on 2026-10-09, by the shape of its answers only. What the check found is in the provider's header and decides the code: `count` is not a total to end a listing by (it came as the total, as the size of the page and as 10,000), so a listing ends at a page that is not full; a search gives 10,000 results at most and the page after them answers an error; the last day asked is included and the newest comes first; a request the API does not like answers 500 with the words of an outage; a registration's number is matched exactly, letter included (`oabNumberKey`: "12345A" is another lawyer than "12345"); a text may be a whole HTML page; the limit is 20 calls per window per address. A listing is whole or it is an error: one that holds more than a listing reads (more than a search gives, or than the calls and the time of one turn: about 6,000 results) says so (`DjenWindowTooLargeError`) and the capture asks for fewer days, never again for the same; what cannot be asked at all (a registration or a day that is not one) is refused (`DjenInvalidQueryError`), never answered as days with nothing; and nothing addressed to the registration is dropped by the client: what cannot be kept is refused and counted by the database. A registration is one number however it is typed (0003: no zeros before it, in the function and in the table), so no second spelling of a lawyer's number reads that lawyer's publications. Migration `0028_publications` (40 pgTAP assertions with two firms): `djen_captures`, one per registration at the bar held by anybody of a firm (whatever their function), keyed by the firm and the number so that a registration removed and added again goes on from where it was (`sync_djen_captures`, `prepare_djen_captures`); a reading that stops because nobody of the firm holds the registration anymore is written in the firm's record, named on the screen and counted on the home while that lawyer is still on record in a lawsuit the court has; claimed under a number, the one that waits the longest first (`claim_djen_captures`); read by who holds the claim (`djen_capture_to_read`) a few days at a time and recorded as it goes (`record_djen_capture`: `partial` keeps what was read and where the reading stopped, `done` only at a day that was read, which the worker names, `paused`, `retry`, `failed`); each round reads a few days again and once a week thirty (a court may cancel late); one communication the database cannot keep is left out alone, counted beside the reading (`last_skipped`) and kept by what tells it apart (`skipped_items`: its hash, its court, its day), and a day that alone holds more than a listing reads stops the reading at that day and says which. `publications`: each communication once per firm under the DJEN's hash, with the plain start and end of its text for the lists (never the marks of a page), linked to the lawsuit of the firm with that case number from the lawsuit's side (triggers on `proceedings`: also when the number comes later, let go when it is corrected or the lawsuit is removed), told in its case's timeline; a court's cancelling is kept with its reason while what was first published stays, and when somebody had already triaged it the firm is told until a person says they saw it (`cancel_pending`, `acknowledge_publication_cancel`). A person who signs or runs the firm's deadlines says what each one is (`triage_publication`: prazo, audiência, decisão, pagamento, ciência, outro; audited, never a support visit) and may ask for a reading now (`request_djen_capture`, once in a while, decided by the database). The AI classifies in shadow mode (task `publicacao.triagem`) what was captured in the last days (`triage_days`): shadow mode is a property of a task (`ai_tasks.shadow`, 0016): an answer of such a task waits in no queue whatever its autonomy says, and its runs are not the firm's to read (the policy of `ai_runs`), so the answer kept beside a publication, in columns no member is granted, is read nowhere else either; a failure that is not about the publication (the provider is out, a key was refused) gives the try back instead of closing it, and a request the provider itself keeps refusing for one publication gives that one up after a few times; the operator compares the AI's word with the people's (`admin_publication_triage_measure`), puts back what the AI gave up on (`admin_reopen_publication_triage`) and reads how the reading is doing across every firm, registrations no sweep has seen included, which is how a sweep that stopped shows (`admin_djen_health`), all in `/admin/ai`. `firm_today` counts the publications to triage, the ones cancelled after their triage and the registrations whose reading is late (or unseen for that long), left a communication out, or stopped for a lawyer still on record. The app: `lib/courts/djen.ts` (the reading is a switch, `DJEN_CAPTURE`: on in production, off elsewhere unless set, because an invented registration may be a real lawyer's number), `lib/publications/*` (the pure rules: a court's text read as plain text in one pass and never as markup, with where a link leads written beside its words, struck words said as struck and what the page hides left out; the days of a search, the load of a call, the health of a reading, the list a person works through; the capture worker and the job `one-djen-capture`, every 5 minutes and on `one/publications.capture.requested`, one registration at a time, each claimed and read in the same step so that no claim goes stale in the job's line; the triage worker and the job `one-publication-triage`, which claims nothing where no model can be asked), the screen `/publicacoes` (what nobody triaged yet, the oldest first, with the start and the end of each text and the six answers one click away; the list is held by the screen from the person's first answer on, so a triaged one stays in place until the person asks for the next ones, whatever the server reads meanwhile; a search by case number that never goes in the address; a page past the end of its list is its last page; how far the reading went per registration, what it left out with the certificate of each at the DJEN, and the readings that stopped, said before the list) and the three counts in `/inicio`, shown to who triages. The workers have their own test against the embedded database (`apps/web/src/lib/publications/capture.db.test.ts`, part of `npm run test:db:roundtrip`). Known limits, for the owner to weigh: the reading is by registration, so when a lawyer leaves a firm nothing of their open lawsuits is read for that firm any longer (the firm is told, and must put the registration back or another lawyer on record), and a lawyer who joins another firm brings the publications of their old cases to the new firm's list (public data of the gazette); a cancelling more than thirty days after the publication is not learned; one day with more than about 6,000 communications for one registration is beyond what one address may read of the public API, and the reading stops there and says so; one lane at the API's 20 calls per window serves about 2,800 registrations at four readings a day, and the shadow triage some 7,000 publications a day; nothing here ran from the deployed app yet, and the API counts calls per address (the region the jobs run in is a decision of the deploy).
- **M2, sprint S7, third part (the piece of a lawsuit, the verified library and the dossier) is built.** `packages/knowledge` is back, as pure rules: `extractCitations` reads what a text cites (a law by number or by name, an article with the norm it is of, a decree, a resolution, a súmula, a theme, a decision by its class and number, a case of a court below by its CNJ number, an enunciado, and the norms that are no law: a provisional measure, an amendment, an ordinance, a regulation) and gives each the key it has in a firm's library (`sourceKey`, the same string `legal_source_key` writes in the database, and `canonicalCitation`, the same words `legal_source_citation` writes); **it fails closed**: what is written as a citation and read into no source (a state law, a reporter's block with no decision beside it, an appeal by a number that is not a CNJ one) is a citation of the kind `ilegivel`, which no library answers for; `citationGap` says why a citation cannot be checked as it is written (an article with no norm, a súmula or a decision of a class several courts have with no court, a decree with no year, a form it does not read); `signature` and `similarity` measure how alike two pieces are (MinHash over sequences of five words, every figure read as one word). Migration `0029_drafts` (63 pgTAP assertions with two firms): the firm's **library** (`legal_sources`: a source a partner or a lawyer checked where it was published, said once, with the address it was checked at; retired with a reason) and its **models of pieces** (`draft_templates`, written by who signs); the **draft** of the piece of a lawsuit not filed yet (`case_drafts`, its versions and what each cites): anybody who works on the case asks for it from a model (`request_case_draft`: the AI writes the first text, task `caso.peca.minuta`, or the model itself is the start; one draft in course per lawsuit), a partner or a lawyer corrects it (`save_draft_version`, which takes the version the text was written on and refuses one that is no longer the latest, `draft_changed`) and approves it with the second factor. **A piece is approved in one way: on its case, as the text a lawyer just read.** `preview_case_draft` answers the text as its document will say it (the facts of the lawsuit written in), its version and its mark (`hash`), with what keeps it from being approved; `approve_case_draft(draft, version, hash)` approves that very text and nothing else, whatever the task's autonomy, and the firm's queue approves no piece (`approve_on_case`; turning the AI's text down there still works, and approving on the case decides the answer that waits, as corrected). **The gate is the database's** (`case_draft_state_blocker`, then `case_draft_text_blocker` on the filled text): the citations of the latest version were read, every one is in the firm's library (an article by an entry of that very article; `case_draft_sources` is what the screens show of it, so they show what the gate reads), no mark is unknown, nothing is left to fill in (`[COMPLETAR: ...]`), and no character is one the PDF cannot write (`unwritable_characters`, the same set as `@flyee/documents/text-pdf`, kept together by a test). The facts of a lawsuit are named in a model by marks (`[[PARTES]]`, `[[NOMES_DAS_PARTES]]`, `[[RE]]`, `[[JUIZO]]`, `[[VALOR_DA_CAUSA]]`, `[[PEDIDO_POR_PARTE]]`, `[[ADVOGADOS]]`) the database fills (`case_draft_filled`), so the AI is never handed a CPF or an address; what the client told reaches the model in the order of the facts the firm approved, and the firm's model as it is, each with the names of the people of the case (whole, or each name alone, however it is written: "Joao" is João) and anything written as an e-mail, a CPF, a CEP or a telephone taken out by the database (`case_text_for_ai`) and once more where the request is written (`maskIdentifiers`); the library goes within a budget, the sources the model of the piece cites first (`draftLibrary`). The backend owes a draft one work at a time (`one-case-drafts`, every 5 minutes and on `one/case.draft.requested`; a numbered claim; claim and work in the same step): the first text (the database reads it from the run, never from the worker; each try after a failed or unusable answer is another run), the reading of the citations with the signature of the text (where the firm's most alike pieces of other cases are measured, `alike`: shown, deciding nothing), and the approved text as a PDF of the firm's own (`renderTextPdf` with the firm as its producer: nothing in the file names the platform), kept under the purpose `petition` and made the petition of its case in the one step that keeps the file. The reading and the document are works of a moment, done before the person who caused them is answered (`settleDraftWork`); only the AI's first text waits for the jobs. Every state has its way forward: a work given up is put back by a person (`resume_case_draft_work`), an approval whose document was not made is taken back by who signs (`reopen_case_draft`, never for a lawsuit already filed or a case that ended), a draft given up keeps its text for reading, an answer of the AI left waiting for a draft that no longer waits for it leaves the queue at the next sweep (`claim_case_drafts`), an answer approved as the AI wrote it is measured as unedited, and a draft follows its lawsuit (one that was filed, closed or removed, or whose case ended, gives up the draft it had in course and withdraws what waited of it); the document an issued piece became is accepted or refused again by a partner or a lawyer only. The **dossier** of a case (`dossiers`, `request_dossier`, `record_dossier`; `lib/cases/dossier.ts` is the pure rule that writes it): who each person is and what the federal registry answered for their CPF, each answer by its own name; what they did on the firm's link, accepted and signed (a signature the provider reported is said as the provider's word with the hours it gave, one a person of the firm confirmed from a file as the firm's, never the second as the first); what they told and confirmed, what was tried before suing and the documents the firm accepted; every moment in one zone named in the document (Brasília), what is not on record said as such, the CPF shown only by its middle; issued as a document of the firm with its public code (kind `dossie`). **A file is taken back record first** (`takeBackFile` in `@flyee/documents/files`, used by every worker that writes one): a worker may believe a file is nobody's after the database made it somebody's document and the answer got lost, so `discard_file` (redefined in 0029) answers `in_use` for a file any table names, and bytes are removed only after their record was forgotten. A long answer of the Anthropic provider is streamed (`max_tokens` above 4,096), stopped by the provider's own clock at 240 seconds, and what an answer cut on the way was charged is still recorded. The app: the card "Peças" on the case (`casos/[id]/components/drafts-card.tsx`: each lawsuit with the one thing its piece needs next; the editor with what the AI asked a lawyer to check, the marks, each source the version cites by its own name and whether the library answers for it, one click from registering it, the firm's most alike pieces; the preview, where the approval happens (or, since 2026-10-27, "Aprovar sem revisar" on the card, through the same gate); the editor never closes over text nobody saved without asking, stays open whatever the page reads meanwhile, and keeps what was typed in the tab, offered back when the draft is opened again), the card "Dossiê do caso" (`dossier-card.tsx`), `/biblioteca` (what the models cite that the library does not answer for yet, each one click from being registered; the models; the sources, read in pages and searched; `?fonte=` opens the dialog on a source somebody came to register), the card of a first text in `/aprovacoes` (it leads to the case and approves nothing), the count of pieces that wait for a lawyer in `/inicio` and the filter `?ver=pecas` in `/casos`; the case screen keeps the last read of a case when a refresh fails; `lib/cases/drafts.ts` holds the pure rules, `draft-actions.ts` the writes, `drafts-work.ts` the backend's half, `draft-facts.ts` what the screens read, `lib/library/*` the library. The worker has its own test against the embedded database, with the model told what to write and the bucket in memory (`apps/web/src/lib/cases/drafts.db.test.ts`, part of `npm run test:db:roundtrip`), the lost answer included. Known limits, for the owner to weigh: the gate blocks what the reader reads and what it knows it cannot read; a citation in a form it does not notice at all is not seen (the forms are tables in `packages/knowledge/src/citations.test.ts`), and one it refuses with no way to register (a state or municipal law, an enunciado of a Turma Recursal, an appeal of a court below by a number that is not a CNJ one) is written by its CNJ number, taken out, or the piece is written outside ONE and added to the case as a document of kind `petition`, which has no gate; the library starts empty, and a list of sources suggested by the platform would be a decision of the owner's; a person's marital status is written with no word that changes with a gender ("pessoa casada"), since none is on record; the client's own words reach the model, with names and identifiers masked and nothing else changed, and a name of somebody who is not of the case (a model written from a real petition) cannot be told from any other word, so only what is written as an identifier is taken out of it; a person whose name has a character the standard fonts of a PDF do not write (Ł, ş, ễ) cannot have a piece approved in ONE (the preview names the character; the piece is then written outside ONE and added as a petition); a stored copy (`normalized`, `signed`) that ends up named by nothing is swept by no job yet.
- **M3, the case's course, in progress on the branch `one/m3`. Its first part (the flight checked against the official record) is built.** Which source, for what, was evaluated on 2026-10-10 (`packages/flights/README.md`): ANAC's VRA is the source of every leg (official, free, what the airline itself reported, cancellations included; a month comes out about five weeks after it ends, a flight entirely abroad is never in it, and every time is Brasília's, taken at the blocks), FlightAware's AeroAPI answers what the VRA cannot yet (a recent flight, a leg abroad), asked by a person, and Flightradar24 is not used (no scheduled time and no cancellation, so it cannot say a flight was late or cancelled). `packages/flights` gains `vra.ts` (a month's file read by the names of its columns, each line a flight or a leg no schedule foresaw; the files of 2021 on in UTF-8, the older ones in Latin-1 with the airline's justification filled in), `providers/anac-vra.ts` (the month's file read as it arrives, never held whole, a month read whole or said not to be; a fake and a contract test beside it), `check.ts` (`conferencia-voo-v1`: which flight of the record is the leg's, by the airline's own number, a codeshare, or the route for an airline the platform does not know, followed through its stops under the same number to the leg's destination, so the stop's arrival is never the leg's; the legs of the same flight no schedule foresaw, by the codes of Portaria nº 1.190/SRE: an included leg, a return to the origin, an extra leg, a leg carried by another flight; and how the case's times stand against a source) and `providers/flightaware.ts` (written from AeroAPI's OpenAPI 4.17.1, read on 2026-10-10; a flight joined through its stop too, each pair of times of one kind, and "no longer tracked" never a cancellation of a flight that left). LATAM's "LA" is read as the five airlines that sell under it (Brasil, Chile, Peru, Paraguay, Ecuador), and FlightAware is asked under each of their ICAO designators in order, LATAM Brasil first, until one knows the flight (AeroAPI's reference asks for an ICAO ident: an IATA one is ambiguous). Migration `0030_flight_checks` (17 pgTAP assertions with two firms): every leg with a flight number and a scheduled departure owes a check of the record (`flight_checks`, kept in step by a trigger on `flight_segments`: a leg whose flight, route or day changed starts over); the backend downloads each month it needs once and reads it as it arrives (`one-flight-checks`, every 30 minutes and on `one/case.flights.changed`; a numbered claim; a month not out yet is asked again later, one that never came is a check given up that says so, never "not found", a month the run did not reach costs no attempt); a person asks the record again (on the record of the firm), or asks FlightAware for a leg the record cannot answer (a daily ceiling per firm counted under a lock of the firm, a wait per leg, each lookup numbered so a late answer is never kept as another's, and a lookup whose answer never came back asked again after a few minutes; `FLIGHTAWARE_API_KEY` in the server's environment). The trip card of a case shows, under each leg, what the record says (agrees, differs, cancelled; its times in each airport's own time, Brasília's said as such; the flight as the record names it when the ticket was a codeshare; the legs no schedule foresaw and, for a cancelled flight, when its aircraft still reached the destination by them, never who was on board), takes the record's real times into the case with one click, and says what FlightAware answered as a private source; the dossier says what the record says of each leg. Checked on the real file of August 2026: 85,757 flights and 1,717 legs no schedule foresaw read in about 2 seconds, every line accounted for, and 191 of its 2,560 cancelled flights had their aircraft reach the destination by such legs. The worker has its own test against the embedded database (`apps/web/src/lib/flights/checks.db.test.ts`, part of `npm run test:db:roundtrip`). Known limits, for the owner to weigh: whether ONE's use of FlightAware (software sold to firms, which reads it for them) fits its Standard plan or needs Premium (from US$ 1,000 a month) is a question for FlightAware's sales before a key goes to production; the record's column of the airline's justification came empty on every line of 2026 read (the files of 2016 and 2018 carry it, and the screen shows it when there is one), so for recent flights the record says what happened and never why; codes 1 and E of the record's legs are not in the text of the Portaria read, and are shown as another leg no schedule foresaw; each Brazilian airport's offset is fixed by its state, so a leg of the summer times before 2019 is compared an hour off (such a claim is past the five years of the consumer code).
- **M3, sprint S8, first part (the course of the lawsuits from the courts) is built.** `packages/courts` gains `ProceedingSource` (`read(cnj)`: a lawsuit and every movement of it, by its number alone, never with a lawyer's login; not found, or an error, never a lawsuit with no movements when it was not read), with three real sources: TJGO's Projudi by its public webservice (`providers/projudi.ts`, operations 74 to 77 at `projudi.tjgo.jus.br/ServicosPublicos`, no key; written from the tribunal's page of automated access and checked against the service on 2026-10-11 by the shape of its answers: Latin-1 XML, an empty list for a number it does not have, no id and no hash for a lawsuit in secret, "Operação Não Autorizada" for an id and a hash that do not match; calls paced one at a time), Escavador's API v2 and Judit's lawsuits API (`providers/escavador.ts`, `providers/judit.ts`, written from their OpenAPI documents read on 2026-10-11, reading each vendor's copy at once; neither ran against its vendor), a small lenient XML reader (`xml.ts`) and the comparative test (`compare.ts`: each source scored against what all of them saw, found, missed, failed, latency, days seen and how far behind; `scripts/compare-sources.ts` runs it by hand on a sample of a firm's lawsuits with the vendors' trial keys, printing counts only). The court's list of the parties is never kept, nor the name of who an intimation is for in what is open, nor any document a vendor's answer carries; a movement a vendor marks private is left out; a movement's own words are kept as the court wrote them (they may name the parties of the firm's own lawsuit). Which aggregator, was researched on 2026-10-11 (`packages/courts/README.md`): Judit (the only public price list; its terms forbid resale without a contract clause), Escavador (its API terms allow a product like ONE; its price is not public) and Codilo (public data only, never a lawyer's login; asynchronous only, so it joins as a source by notice if chosen) for the comparative test; Jusbrasil's Digesto in reserve (its intimations module marks intimations as read with the lawyer's account, which ONE must never turn on); Vigilant follows no lawsuit by its number. Migration `0031_court_tracking` (18 pgTAP assertions with two firms): `proceeding_tracking`, one per lawsuit filed with a number (kept by a trigger on `proceedings`: its source told by the number, TJGO's from Projudi and the others from the aggregator; another number is another lawsuit, and what was read of the old one goes; a lawsuit closed is not read, and one filed again is), with what the court shows of it as last read (`snapshot`), which source read it (`vendor`: another aggregator is another copy, so what the one before read goes and the next reading is a first one), when the source last answered for it (`checked_at`) and the movements nobody of the firm saw yet (`unseen_count`; a lawsuit not read any more keeps none as news); `movements`, each once under the source's own id; the backend's `claim_proceeding_reads` (a numbered claim, from the sources the backend can read now, a person's request first) and `record_proceeding_read` (read, secret, not found and asked again after `not_found_hours`, retry, `release` for a refused key or a balance run out at the aggregator, which gives the lawsuit's attempt back, failed; the first whole reading of a lawsuit is its history and no news, a later one tells the case's timeline, `court_moved`, what it learned; a lawsuit of a case that ended is not read); a person's `request_proceeding_read` (once in a while, audited) and `acknowledge_proceeding_movements` (audited); the setting `tracking` (how often, how long to wait for a lawsuit the court does not show, the hours of Brasília Projudi is read in by routine: 22 to 6, a courtesy to the tribunal, not a rule of its found anywhere); and `firm_today` with the lawsuits that moved and the readings given up. The job `one-court-tracking` (every 10 minutes and on `one/proceedings.read.requested`) reads one lawsuit at a time, claimed and read in the same step; a refused key or a balance run out at the aggregator stops the aggregator for the rest of the run, with no attempt of the lawsuit spent; an answer that cannot be kept (more than 5,000 movements are cut to the latest) is a reading given up that says so; the courts are read where `COURT_TRACKING` is on (production, unless 0), the aggregator the one `COURT_AGGREGATOR` names with its key. The app: under each lawsuit of a case, its course (`casos/[id]/components/court-course.tsx`): when it was last read and from where, its unit, state and judge, what is open in it with its term, its latest movements with the new ones marked, the lawyers on record, "Marcar como visto" and "Ler agora no tribunal", and each state's words (the first reading on its way, a court that does not show it yet, a lawsuit in secret, a reading given up, a court no source reads here yet); `lib/courts/course.ts` holds the pure rules and words, `tracking.ts` the sources, `tracking-work.ts` the backend's half, `course-facts.ts` what the screen reads, `actions.ts` the writes; the tile "Processos com andamento novo" in `/inicio` and the filter `?ver=andamento` in `/casos`. The worker has its own test against the embedded database (`apps/web/src/lib/courts/tracking.db.test.ts`, part of `npm run test:db:roundtrip`). Known limits, for the owner to weigh: the hearings and conclusions of Projudi's operation 76 came empty in every lawsuit seen, so they are read by whatever fields they carry; a registration at the bar is written by Projudi as "12345 A" and its letter's meaning was not found, so a lawyer on record is shown as written and never matched against the firm's registrations yet; Escavador gives a movement's day and no hour; a lawsuit in secret shows nothing of its course to any source that reads by the number alone.
- **M3, sprint S8, second part (the firm's portfolio from a spreadsheet, and the list of cases at its size) is built.** A firm that arrives with lawsuits in court brings them from the spreadsheet of the system it used before (the pilot firm's is Astrea's), never one by one. The file is read in the person's browser and never uploaded (`apps/web/src/lib/import`): an Excel workbook (`xlsx.ts`, with `fflate`: shared and inline strings, dates by the cell's style and the 1904 calendar, the first sheet a person sees (a hidden one is left), elements with or without a namespace's prefix, only the rows with something in them, a ceiling on what a file unpacks to, and a number of more than fifteen digits kept as Excel shows it, never turned into twenty digits that would be another lawsuit's) or a text export (`csv.ts`: UTF-8 or Windows-1252, the delimiter most of its first lines agree on, weighed by the cells it cuts, quoted cells), told by its bytes and never by its name; an old `.xls`, a PDF or a UTF-16 text is named and refused with what to do; the line of the headers is found under a report's title by what its cells name, above the first line holding a lawsuit's number, and the person may say it is another. Which column is which fact is guessed from the names systems give them and confirmed by the person, a column with no header by its letter (`mapping.ts`; the lawsuit's number and the client's name are required), and each line is shown as what it would do before anything is written (`portfolio.ts`: open a case, already in ONE, repeated in the file, or not read and why: no number, a number Excel did not keep whole (with an exponent, or written in full with its last digits zero), a number that does not check, a court ONE does not follow yet, no client; a CPF that does not check, a company's document, a CPF the file gives somebody of another name than the one most of its lines carry, a day not understood, before the setting's oldest (old systems write 01/01/1900 for none) or still to come are left out of a line that goes on), with the first lines as they will enter, how many carry a CPF, an airline, a day and an end, what the old system said of the lawsuits read as ended, and each way the other party is written with the airline it was read as, for the person to correct before anything goes. A lawsuit is matched by its CNJ number (one a spreadsheet took the zeros from is given them back when it then checks), so the same file brought twice opens nothing twice; a client by the CPF only under a name that is theirs (`import_names_match` and `namesMatch`, the same rule: a CPF the firm has for somebody of another name stays theirs, and the line's client is written without it, `cpf_other_name`, listed in the result), keeping the name the firm gave them; the airline by the company's names in court first (`airlines.court_names`: "TAM" and "LATAM Brasil" are LATAM's "LA", "VRG" Gol, "Passaredo" Voepass, each read in the company's public registration on 2026-10-11), then by its own name, a name of one word or an airline's own word only where the party's name says it is an airline ("Azul Companhia de Seguros" is no airline); a party that names two airlines is none, and one of an airline that no longer flies is none too (Avianca Brasil, `O6`, inactive and known by its names, so that "Oceanair (Avianca Brasil)" is never read as Avianca); a lawsuit that ended by the words of the old system that say so (archived, closed, extinguished; never one archived provisionally, suspended, sent back to the court below, nor a final judgment, which still has its enforcement ahead). Migration `0032_portfolio_import` (27 pgTAP assertions with two firms): `import_batches` (with what the screen counted of the whole file) and `import_rows`; a partner, a lawyer or who runs the deadlines (never a support visit) starts a batch, sends the lines a few a call (each checked again by the database: a number of the court named, a name, a CPF that is one, a kind, a day of the calendar between the setting's oldest and today, an airline ONE knows; one line that is not right refuses its call and says which and what of it) and decides them a few a call with the second factor (`commit_import_batch`: each line once, in its order; a lawsuit the firm has is left as it is; another opens a case through `create_case` and `save_proceeding`, filed or closed, with the case at the stage of its lawsuit and `imported` in its timeline; a line that cannot be written, for a reason the database names or one nobody foresaw, is refused alone and leaves nothing). Which of its lawsuits the firm already has is asked in the request's body (`known_lawsuits`, a thousand at a time). A batch is undone a few lines a call (`revert_import_batch`: a case it opened goes when nobody touched it since (`import_case_touched`: nothing in its timeline by a person, a client or the AI, nothing in the audit trail of its lawsuits and people, no CPF of its people looked up), each on record (`import.case_removed`), and with it a client an import wrote, with the last case of theirs; one somebody worked on stays, and the answer says how many; one another spreadsheet not undone found in the firm passes to that one, which undoes it as its own (`handed_over`)). An import that stopped half way brings nothing again from the same conference: the person goes on with it or undoes it, or confers the file again. A batch that stopped half way is continued or undone from the history, which shows every batch a person may still act on; one nobody finished is closed by the daily sweep `one-import-sweep` after `keep_days` without a call (one never started goes; one started is closed as far as it went, its cases staying for a person to undo); a batch that ended keeps each lawsuit's number and what became of it, and nothing the spreadsheet said of people. Every step is on record (`import.started`, `import.lines_added`, `import.committed`, `import.case_removed`, `import.reverted`, `import.discarded`, `import.dropped`, `import.expired`). How many lines a batch holds (20,000) and a call takes, and the oldest day of distribution, are the setting the screen reads (`import_limits`); the browser reads a file to 100,000 rows, and a file longer than a batch is brought in more than one. The app: `/casos/importar` (the file, the columns with an example of each, the conference, and what came of it: the lines refused and why, the ones that came without the CPF, and how many of the file were never sent; the history with continue, undo and discard) and, for who may, a button in `/casos` and the second action of its empty list. **The list of cases** is read a page at a time (migration `0033_case_list`, 17 pgTAP assertions with two firms): `firm_cases` counts every filter, filters (the stages, and what waits for the firm, counted as the home counts it), searches (a client's name by its words in any order and without accents, a CPF, a lawsuit's number whole or by how it starts, the case's own number; a set at a time, never a query per case; with no filter a search looks at the ended cases too) and pages, over partial indexes by the firm, so the numbers are right for a firm of any size; the search travels in a server action's body and never in the address (`lib/cases/list.ts`, `list-read.ts`, `list-actions.ts`), and the app's table gained a server mode for it (`DataTable` `server`: the grid sorts and filters nothing by itself, waits for the person to stop typing, and exports nothing of a page). `create_case` takes the firm's row `for no key update`, so a batch that opens hundreds of cases never holds a message of the firm behind it. The import has its own test against the embedded database, from the bytes of a file to the cases found in the list and undone (`apps/web/src/lib/import/import.db.test.ts`, part of `npm run test:db:roundtrip`, whose files now run one at a time: the backend's sweeps act across firms, so one file's lawsuits are another's sweep's). Known limits, for the owner to weigh: the names Astrea gives its columns were not found published, so the first import confirms each column by hand; a lawsuit of a court ONE does not follow yet (a federal or labour court) does not enter; an imported lawsuit opens a case of the incident "other", its flight to be written by the firm; a party's name the rules read as the wrong airline is corrected by the person in the conference, and the list of the companies' names in court is the operator's to grow; a client with no CPF in the spreadsheet, or whose CPF the firm has under another name, enters once per lawsuit, since nothing tells two people of one name apart.
- **M3, sprint S9, first part (the deadlines, counted over each court's calendar and confirmed by a person) is built.** The pure rule is `@flyee/rules/deadlines` (`prazo-v1`): business days only unless the term is in calendar days (CPC, art. 219; Lei 9.099/1995, art. 12-A), the day it runs from left out and the last counted (art. 224), a communication of the DJEN published on the first business day after it was made available and counted from the business day after that (§§ 2º and 3º; Resolução CNJ 455/2022, art. 11, § 3º, in the words of Resolução CNJ 569/2024, from 16 May 2025 on), no term in business days running from 20 December to 20 January in any court, the small-claims courts too (art. 220; Resolução CNJ 244/2016, art. 3º, read on 2026-10-11), nor on a day the court suspended them, and a last day of partial business moved to the next business day (art. 224, § 1º); the national holidays of federal law are the rule's, the rest of each court's calendar is data. It is conservative: what it cannot know never puts a deadline later than it is, and is said as an assumption for whoever confirms (a year whose calendar nobody checked, a comarca not known or whose own holidays are not on record, a day of the calendar for a kind of lawsuit or a system not known, a partial day at the start, a start day that is no business day, a publication before the DJEN's rule, and a term in calendar days that ran through a suspension of the procedural terms: the rule cannot tell such a term procedural, so it does not stop there, which gives the earlier day, and says that a procedural one is counted in business days and ends later); every day that did not count is named with why, and the count says when each year of the court's calendar it went through was checked against the court's act (a decree after that day is not in it). Migration `0034_deadlines` (30 pgTAP assertions with two firms): the forensic calendar is the platform's (`court_calendar_years`, a year of a court checked against its act, with the act's address, the day it was checked and whose comarcas' own days it holds; `court_calendar_days`, the days a court had no business, suspended the terms or had partial business, for every lawsuit or for some kinds, systems or comarcas), seeded with TJGO's list of holidays of 2026 (docs.tjgo.jus.br, version of 8 June 2026: Carnival with Ash Wednesday until noon, Holy Week, the days off the court decreed, the day of four comarcas, the recess into 2027; of the municipal holidays only Goiânia's are in it), kept by the operator in `/admin/calendario`, and read by one function (`court_calendar`) for the backend's count and for the count a person is shown before proposing, so the two never differ; `deadline_acts` holds the acts a term is usually set for with the days the law gives them, a start for the person and never a decision (an act the operator takes off the list stays the act of the deadlines that have it). A deadline (`deadlines`) is of a lawsuit by its number and court, of the case and the lawsuit when the firm has them, and of the publication it came from (and then follows the lawsuit that publication is of); who signs or runs the deadlines proposes it (`propose_deadline`: from the day the DJEN made the publication available, or from a day they name, of a publication in force or of a lawsuit the court has, filed or closed), the backend counts it with the rule (`one-deadline-counts`, every five minutes and on `one/deadline.count`, and right after a person's write so the screen shows the day at once; a numbered claim, the count read and recorded for the revision in force, never by a person; a claim whose reading or recording failed is given back at once, `release_deadline_count`, and after its tries the deadline waits for a person, who asks for the count again, `retry_deadline_count`), and a partner or the controladoria confirms it on the counted day or another with the reason (`confirm_deadline`, naming the revision whose count they read). Every change of what the count reads is a revision counted again: the inputs, the lawsuit's court, comarca, system or kind, and a change of the court's calendar for the open deadlines whose count goes through the days it changed (a year checked again on a later day, its note or its address, changes no count); a correction of a deadline's words alone keeps its count and its confirmation. A confirmed deadline keeps its day; what a person is asked to look at again is said with why (`deadline_review_reasons`: the court took back its publication, the count now gives another day, or it could not be counted) and stops being asked once who runs the deadlines confirms it knowing it, on the new count or keeping its day with the reason; while a count gives an earlier day than the confirmed one, the deadline is due, past its day and ordered by the earlier (`deadline_urgent_on`). A publication somebody triaged as a deadline and no deadline came from is listed in `/prazos` and counted on the home for 60 days after it was triaged (`watch_days`; a publication read late is watched from when it was read: `firm_publications_without_deadline`), so none is lost between the triage and the count; when its deadline was recorded from the lawsuit (an intimation read in the court's system), a person links that deadline to it (`link_deadline_publication`). The lists read the firm's open and closed deadlines by their own indexes, and only a page's rows whole. Done, dismissed with why, and reopened to be counted and confirmed again; each step audited and told in the case's timeline. The app: `/prazos` (what waits for confirmation first, each deadline with its day, how far it is, how it was counted and what the count could not know, the confirmation one click away; the open deadlines due from today on, the ones past their day, to look at again and concluded, a click on their count), "Calcular prazo" on every publication (and offered right after one is triaged as a deadline), the card "Prazos" on a case with a new deadline of a lawsuit from a day (an intimation read in the court's system, one in a hearing), the counts on `/inicio` (past their day, due soon, to confirm, to look at again, triaged as a deadline with none, each to who works on them; how many days are "soon" is the setting the cards read too) and the operator's `/admin/calendario` (which years of each court were checked, the days of a court and year, the acts of the list). `lib/deadlines/deadlines.ts` holds the pure rules and words, `count-work.ts` the backend's half, `facts.ts` what the screens read, `actions.ts` the writes; `components/deadlines/*` the card, the count and the form. The worker has its own test against the embedded database (`apps/web/src/lib/deadlines/deadlines.db.test.ts`, part of `npm run test:db:roundtrip`), the claim given back and the count asked again included. Known limits, for the owner to weigh: the calendar holds TJGO's 2026 only, so every other court and year counts only weekends, the national holidays and the year's-end suspension, and says so, until the operator registers each court's act; the municipal holidays of a comarca other than Goiânia are not in TJGO's list; a comarca is matched by the name the lawsuit records; a publication of a court that is not a state court (a federal or labour one) has no calendar here; a term in hours (the 48 hours of a preparation) or in months is not counted; a partial day at the start, and a start day that is no business day, are read the way that gives the earlier day, and said.
- **M3, sprint S9, second part (the AI's triage with a sample, and the filing packages of eproc and PJe) is built.** The triage of publications leaves the shadow per category of the AI's answer (migration `0035_triage_sampled`, 21 pgTAP assertions with two firms): `publication_triage_policies` holds each category in `shadow` (the answer decides nothing, as in 0028) or `sampled`, and only the operator moves a category out of the shadow, on the record, when its measure met the goal of the setting `publications` (in the last 90 days, at least 100 publications the AI put in that category and a person triaged without the AI's word in front of them, 97 percent of them agreeing, and none where a person found a deadline or a hearing the AI had not seen; the numbers are data). A deadline, a hearing, a decision and a payment never leave the shadow (`triage_acting`; the database refuses it): what follows their triage, the deadline counted, the hearing kept, the decision read for an appeal, the money told to the client, is a person's work, and a triage nobody saw would leave it undone; what the AI may triage alone is a notice and the rest. In a sampled category the AI's answer is the triage of a publication nobody triaged yet, in a firm that allows it (`organizations.publication_ai_triage`, a partner's switch, off until a partner turns it on), said to be the AI's (`ai_applied`), and a share of them (20 percent unless the operator names another, chosen by the publication's id so a retry never changes it) waits for a person's check (`review_due`); the first person's word that finds a deadline, a hearing, a decision or a payment where the AI said anything else (a check, a person's own triage, or a triage the AI's answer came after, in any firm, whether it lets the AI triage or not; never the same word said again) puts that category back in the shadow by itself, in every firm, and every triage of it the AI made and nobody checked in the last 30 days waits for a person again (one somebody is triaging at that moment is left to them); the person is told only when they were checking the AI's own triage. The AI's answer is read under the policy's lock, so nothing applies after a demotion. A firm reads of each category only whether it is out and its share (never the operator's note). The firm's card of the AI's triage shows in `/publicacoes` once a category is out of the shadow. The task's runs stay out of the firm's sight (`ai_tasks.shadow`): a firm reads the triage, never the AI's word on a category still in the shadow. The app: in `/publicacoes` the list "Conferir" (the AI's triage that waits for a check, with "Está certo" one click away or the right answer), the AI's mark on what it triaged and the firm's switch for a partner; in `/admin/ai` the card of the categories (`triage-policy-card.tsx`: the goal, each category's measure against it, letting one out with its sample or putting it back, with the decision on record); `firm_today` counts the triage to check. The filing packages of eproc and PJe (migration `0036_filing_systems`, 8 pgTAP assertions with two firms; `admin_set_court_system` redefined, so a system that changes leaves the petition's limit and the types of the one before): `court_systems` gains the petition's own limit (`max_petition_bytes`, for a system that takes the petition apart from the documents) and the type the lawyer picks for each kind of file (`file_types`), and a package records the petition's limit it was built under; seeded from the courts' own manuals, each unconfirmed until the test of the station: the eproc of TJSP and TJSC with 11 MB per PDF, the PJe of TJCE with a petition of up to 1.5 MB, and the petition's type as each system names it ("Petição inicial (INIC)" in eproc, "Petição inicial" in PJe). The pure rule (`planFiling`) never cuts a petition that has a limit of its own: it is measured whole against the smaller of that limit and the limit of a file, and one above it is a problem (`petition_too_large`); the database refuses a package whose petition is cut or over its limit, and a petition's own limit above the limit of a file. The card "Protocolo" says per system what the lawyer does there, each step once (eproc: the lawsuit's information with the parties and the value, documents, confirm; PJe: initial data, subjects, parties and characteristics, petitions and documents, file), the type of each file where the system names it, and, in PJe, the description each attachment asks for. Known limits, for the owner to weigh: the types of the files other than the petition are picked by the lawyer from the court's list until the test of the station reads them; the limits of the eproc and PJe of every other court are not known until that test; only a notice and "other" can be let out of the shadow, and letting more out is the owner's to weigh; a firm that does not turn the AI's triage on triages every publication by hand, as before; when most categories are let out, a publication still untriaged after the AI read it tells a member that the AI's word was one still in the shadow (never which).
- **M3, sprint S10, first part (the hearings, and the firm's calendar in Google) is built.** `packages/calendar` is the adapter of a firm's calendar (`CalendarProvider`: the consent, the grant, `createCalendar`, `calendarAccess` (whether the account may still write a calendar, by its role in the account's list), `upsertEvent`, `deleteEvent`, `revoke`; `eventIdOf`, the event's id of a hearing, its uuid in base32hex, so writing a hearing again replaces its event and brings back one taken out; a fake that consents at once, lenient on a preview of more than one instance, and the Google provider written from Google's references read on 2026-10-11: OAuth 2.0 for web server applications with `access_type=offline` and `prompt=consent select_account`, the one scope `calendar.app.created`, which makes secondary calendars and touches only their events, never the account's other calendars, and the Calendar API's own errors in their old and new forms, what is the platform's own told apart from any firm's (its project out of its day, `dailyLimitExceeded`; its client refused, `invalid_client`; the API off in its project), so it never stops a firm's calendar; `sendUpdates=none` and no guests, so no e-mail goes to anybody). Migration `0037_hearings` (26 pgTAP assertions with two firms): each court has the zone of its state (`courts.time_zone`: Acre and the west of Amazonas five hours behind Greenwich, the rest of Amazonas, Roraima, Rondônia and the Mato Grossos four, the rest three, by Lei 12.876/2013, with no summer time since Decreto 9.772/2019) and the zones some of its comarcas keep besides (`courts.other_zones`: TJAM the Acre time, TJPE Fernando de Noronha's), and a hearing (`hearings`) of a filed lawsuit is the moment it starts, in the zone it is said in (the court's; for a court whose comarcas keep more than one, the one the person says, `zone_required`, since the law draws the Acre time in Amazonas by a line and names no municipality, and the lists found disagree), its kind (conciliação, instrução e julgamento, una, outra), how it happens (in person, by videoconference, or both, with the place and the link), who attends (somebody of the firm, or a name from outside it), where it came from (a person, or the publication that set it) and what came of it (held with or without a settlement, cancelled with why; put back to be held when said by mistake); one lawsuit has one hearing at a moment. Who signs or runs the deadlines writes it (`save_hearing`, `set_hearing_status`, audited and told in the case's timeline, never a support visit; the answer says whether the firm's calendar takes it). The firm's calendar (`firm_calendars`: active, lost by itself with why, or off when a person cut it, which tells the firm nothing): who manages the firm connects a Google account in `/settings/connections` (the consent's state kept in a cookie of the person's own start, the grant sealed in the vault, and the firm's calendar taken again when the account may still write it and it was not deleted in Google, a new one made otherwise; never a second one on a guess, `lib/hearings/calendar-take.ts`), takes back the calendar the firm had under the grant in hand when connecting could not (`retakeCalendar`), may make a new calendar whenever the account is connected (confirmed when one is in use), connects again when Google no longer honours the grant (said to the firm as the worker says it), and cuts it after a confirmation that says what stays; the backend writes each hearing to it a revision at a time (`one-hearing-calendar`, every ten minutes and on `one/hearings.changed`, asked right after a person's write and never waited for; the grant opened once per connection and round; a numbered claim; whatever came of a revision no longer in force spends nothing of the one in force, and a hearing cancelled while it was written is still taken out; a grant opened before the account was connected again stops nothing of the calendar in use); a hearing that goes with its lawsuit or its case owes the removal of its event (`hearing_event_removals`, the backend's own list, taken out by the sweep or left on record; one being written is taken out of the calendar in use once the write had its time to land; owed nothing for a firm that goes); a grant that no longer holds, a calendar deleted in Google or a connection that expired stops the calendar (`firm_calendar_lost`), said to who manages the firm with its one way back (connect again, make the calendar again), and nothing is retried meanwhile; what is the platform's own stops the round with every try given back and no firm's calendar stopped. An event names nobody of the case (the kind, the case's number, the lawsuit, the time in its zone, how and where, who of the firm goes, and the case's address in ONE): a calendar is shared and shows on phones, and a name written there would outlive a correction or an erasure. The app: `/agenda` (what is coming first, each hearing with when in its zone, how, where or by which link, who goes, and how its event stands in Google; from its start what came of it may be said, and once it lasted its time it is said to have passed; the ones past their time with no word on them, held and cancelled, a click on their count), the card "Audiências" on a case, "Registrar audiência" on a publication triaged as a hearing (offered right after the triage, as a deadline is), the counts on `/inicio` (hearings in the next seven days and past their time with no word on them, to who works on deadlines; a calendar that stopped, to who manages the firm) and the menu's "Agenda". `lib/hearings/hearings.ts` holds the pure rules, the words and the event of a hearing (`eventOf`), `facts.ts` what the screens read, `actions.ts` and `calendar-actions.ts` the writes, `calendar-work.ts` the backend's half and `jobs.ts` its job; `lib/calendar/provider.ts` chooses Google (`GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` and `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET`) or the fake (`CALENDAR_FAKE=1`, never in production); the consent comes back to `/api/connections/google/callback`. The worker has its own test against the embedded database, with the vault sealing for real and Google standing in (`apps/web/src/lib/hearings/hearings.db.test.ts`, part of `npm run test:db:roundtrip`).
